Pergunta 2

Base directory for this skill: [oculto]/.claude/skills/video-use

Video Use

Principle

  1. LLM reasons from raw transcript + on-demand visuals. The only derived artifact that earns its keep is a packed phrase-level transcript (takes_packed.md). Everything else — filler tagging, retake detection, shot classification, emphasis scoring — you derive at decision time.
  2. Audio is primary, visuals follow. Cut candidates come from speech boundaries and silence gaps. Drill into visuals only at decision points.
  3. Ask → confirm → execute → iterate → persist. Never touch the cut until the user has confirmed the strategy in plain English.
  4. Generalize. Do not assume what kind of video this is. Look at the material, ask the user, then edit.
  5. Artistic freedom is the default. Every specific value, preset, font, color, duration, pitch structure, and technique in this document is a worked example from one proven video — not a mandate. Read them to understand what's possible and why each worked. Then make your own taste calls based on what the material actually is and what the user actually wants. The only things you MUST do are in the Hard Rules section below. Everything else is yours.
  6. Invent freely. If the material calls for a technique not described here — split-screen, picture-in-picture, lower-third identity cards, reaction cuts, speed ramps, freeze frames, crossfades, match cuts, L-cuts, J-cuts, speed ramps over breath, whatever — build it. The helpers are ffmpeg and PIL. They can do anything the format supports. Do not wait for permission.
  7. Verify your own output before showing it to the user. If you wouldn't ship it, don't present it.

Hard Rules (production correctness — non-negotiable)

These are the things where deviation produces silent failures or broken output. They are not taste, they are correctness. Memorize them.

  1. Subtitles are applied LAST in the filter chain, after every overlay. Otherwise overlays hide captions. Silent failure.
  2. Per-segment extract → lossless -c copy concat, not single-pass filtergraph. Otherwise you double-encode every segment when overlays are added.
  3. 30ms audio fades at every segment boundary (afade=t=in:st=0:d=0.03,afade=t=out:st={dur-0.03}:d=0.03). Otherwise audible pops at every cut.
  4. Overlays use setpts=PTS-STARTPTS+T/TB to shift the overlay's frame 0 to its window start. Otherwise you see the middle of the animation during the overlay window.
  5. Master SRT uses output-timeline offsets: output_time = word.start - segment_start + segment_offset. Otherwise captions misalign after segment concat.
  6. Never cut inside a word. Snap every cut edge to a word boundary from the Scribe transcript.
  7. Pad every cut edge. Working window: 30–200ms. Scribe timestamps drift 50–100ms — padding absorbs the drift. Tighter for fast-paced, looser for cinematic.
  8. Word-level verbatim ASR only. Never SRT/phrase mode (loses sub-second gap data). Never normalized fillers (loses editorial signal).
  9. Cache transcripts per source. Never re-transcribe unless the source file itself changed.
  10. Parallel sub-agents for multiple animations. Never sequential. Spawn N at once via the Agent tool; total wall time ≈ slowest one.
  11. Strategy confirmation before execution. Never touch the cut until the user has approved the plain-English plan.
  12. All session outputs in <videos_dir>/edit/. Never write inside the video-use/ project directory.

Everything else in this document is a worked example. Deviate whenever the material calls for it.

Directory layout

The skill lives in video-use/. User footage lives wherever they put it. All session outputs go into <videos_dir>/edit/.

<videos_dir>/
├── <source files, untouched>
└── edit/
    ├── project.md               ← memory; appended every session
    ├── takes_packed.md          ← phrase-level transcripts, the LLM's primary reading view
    ├── edl.json                 ← cut decisions
    ├── transcripts/<name>.json  ← cached raw Scribe JSON
    ├── animations/slot_<id>/    ← per-animation source + render + reasoning
    ├── clips_graded/            ← per-segment extracts with grade + fades
    ├── master.srt               ← output-timeline subtitles
    ├── downloads/               ← yt-dlp outputs
    ├── verify/                  ← debug frames / timeline PNGs
    ├── preview.mp4
    └── final.mp4

Setup

First-time install lives in install.md (clone, deps, ffmpeg, skill registration, API key). Don't re-run it every session; on cold start just verify:

  • ELEVENLABS_API_KEY resolves — either in the environment or in .env at the video-use repo root. If missing, ask the user to paste one and write it to .env (never to the user's <videos_dir>).
  • ffmpeg + ffprobe on PATH.
  • Python deps installed (uv sync or pip install -e . inside the repo).
  • Node.js + npm available if the session needs HyperFrames or Remotion slots. HyperFrames currently requires Node.js 22+.
  • yt-dlp, HyperFrames, Remotion, Manim installed only on first use.
  • First-use animation setup happens inside the slot directory, never at the video-use repo root. HyperFrames can be invoked with npx --yes hyperframes ...; Remotion can be scaffolded with npx create-video@latest or installed as a project-local dependency before using its remotion render command.
  • This skill vendors skills/manim-video/. Read its SKILL.md when building a Manim slot.

Helpers (helpers/transcribe.py, helpers/render.py, etc.) live alongside this SKILL.md. Resolve their paths relative to the directory containing this file — the skill is typically symlinked at ~/.claude/skills/video-use/ or ~/.codex/skills/video-use/.

Helpers

  • transcribe.py <video> — single-file Scribe call. --num-speakers N optional. Cached.
  • transcribe_batch.py <videos_dir> — 4-worker parallel transcription. Use for multi-take.
  • pack_transcripts.py --edit-dir <dir> — transcripts/*.json → takes_packed.md (phrase-level, break on silence ≥ 0.5s).
  • timeline_view.py <video> <start> <end> — filmstrip + waveform PNG. On-demand visual drill-down. Not a scan tool — use it at decision points, not constantly.
  • render.py <edl.json> -o <out> — per-segment extract → concat → overlays (PTS-shifted) → subtitles LAST. --preview for 720p fast. --build-subtitles to generate master.srt inline.
  • grade.py <in> -o <out> — ffmpeg filter chain grade. Presets + --filter '<raw>' for custom.

For animations, create <edit>/animations/slot_<id>/ with Bash and spawn a sub-agent via the Agent tool.

The process

  1. Inventory. ffprobe every source. transcribe_batch.py on the directory. pack_transcripts.py to produce takes_packed.md. Sample one or two timeline_views for a visual first impression.
  2. Pre-scan for problems. One pass over takes_packed.md to note verbal slips, obvious mis-speaks, or phrasings to avoid. Plain list, feed into the editor brief.
  3. Converse. Describe what you see in plain English. Ask questions shaped by the material. Collect: content type, target length/aspect, aesthetic/brand direction, pacing feel, must-preserve moments, must-cut moments, animation and grade preferences, subtitle needs. Do not use a fixed checklist — the right questions are different every time.
  4. Propose strategy. 4–8 sentences: shape, take choices, cut direction, animation plan, grade direction, subtitle style, length estimate. Wait for confirmation.
  5. Execute. Produce edl.json via the editor sub-agent brief. Drill into timeline_view at ambiguous moments. Build animations in parallel sub-agents. Apply grade per-segment. Compose via render.py.
  6. Preview. render.py --preview.
  7. Self-eval (before showing the user). Run timeline_view on the rendered output (not the sources) at every cut boundary (±1.5s window). Check each image for:
  8. Visual discontinuity / flash / jump at the cut
  9. Waveform spike at the boundary (audio pop that slipped past the 30ms fade)
  10. Subtitle hidden behind an overlay (Rule 1 violation)
  11. Overlay misaligned or showing wrong frames (Rule 4 violation)

Also sample: first 2s, last 2s, and 2–3 mid-points — check grade consistency, subtitle readability, overall coherence. Run ffprobe on the output to verify duration matches the EDL expectation.

If anything fails: fix → re-render → re-eval. Cap at 3 self-eval passes — if issues remain after 3, flag them to the user rather than looping forever. Only present the preview once the self-eval passes.

  1. Iterate + persist. Natural-language feedback, re-plan, re-render. Never re-transcribe. Final render on confirmation. Append to project.md.

Cut craft (techniques)

  • Audio-first. Candidate cuts from word boundaries and silence gaps.
  • Preserve peaks. Laughs, punchlines, emphasis beats. Extend past punchlines to include reactions — the laugh IS the beat.
  • Speaker handoffs benefit from air between utterances. Common values: 400–600ms. Less for fast-paced, more for cinematic. Taste call.
  • Audio events as signals. (laughs), (sighs), (applause) mark beats. Extend past them.
  • Silence gaps are cut candidates. Silences ≥400ms are usually the cleanest. 150–400ms phrase boundaries are usable with a visual check. <150ms is unsafe (mid-phrase).
  • Example cut padding (the launch video shipped with this): 50ms before the first kept word, 80ms after the last. Tighter for montage energy, looser for documentary. Stay in the 30–200ms working window (Hard Rule 7).
  • Never reason audio and video independently. Every cut must work on both tracks.

The packed transcript (primary reading view)

pack_transcripts.py reads all transcripts/*.json and produces one markdown file where each take is a list of phrase-level lines, each prefixed with its [start-end] time range. Phrases break on any silence ≥ 0.5s OR speaker change. This is the artifact the editor sub-agent reads to pick cuts — it gives word-boundary precision from text alone at 1/10 the tokens of raw JSON.

Example line:

## C0103  (duration: 43.0s, 8 phrases)
  [002.52-005.36] S0 Ninety percent of what a web agent does is completely wasted.
  [006.08-006.74] S0 We fixed this.

Editor sub-agent brief (for multi-take selection)

When the task is "pick the best take of each beat across many clips," spawn a dedicated sub-agent with a brief shaped like this. The structure is load-bearing; the pitch-shape example is not.

You are editing a <type> video. Pick the best take of each beat and 
assemble them chronologically by beat, not by source clip order.

INPUTS:
  - takes_packed.md (time-annotated phrase-level transcripts of all takes)
  - Product/narrative context: <2 sentences from the user>
  - Speaker(s): <name, role, delivery style note>
  - Expected structure: <pick an archetype or invent one>
  - Verbal slips to avoid: <list from the pre-scan pass>
  - Target runtime: <seconds>

Common structural archetypes (pick, adapt, or invent):
  - Tech launch / demo:   HOOK → PROBLEM → SOLUTION → BENEFIT → EXAMPLE → CTA
  - Tutorial:             INTRO → SETUP → STEPS → GOTCHAS → RECAP
  - Interview:            (QUESTION → ANSWER → FOLLOWUP) repeat
  - Travel / event:       ARRIVAL → HIGHLIGHTS → QUIET MOMENTS → DEPARTURE
  - Documentary:          THESIS → EVIDENCE → COUNTERPOINT → CONCLUSION
  - Music / performance:  INTRO → VERSE → CHORUS → BRIDGE → OUTRO
  - Or invent your own.

RULES:
  - Start/end times must fall on word boundaries from the transcript.
  - Pad cut boundaries (working window 30–200ms).
  - Prefer silences ≥ 400ms as cut targets.
  - Unavoidable slips are kept if no better take exists. Note them in "reason".
  - If over budget, revise: drop a beat or trim tails. Report total and self-correct.

OUTPUT (JSON array, no prose):
  [{"source": "C0103", "start": 2.42, "end": 6.85, "beat": "HOOK",
    "quote": "...", "reason": "..."}, ...]

Return the final EDL and a one-line total runtime check.

Color grade (when requested)

Your job is to reason about the image, not apply a preset. Look at a frame (via timeline_view), decide what's wrong, adjust one thing, look again.

Mental model is ASC CDL. Per channel: out = (in * slope + offset) ** power, then global saturation. slope → highlights, offset → shadows, power → midtones.

Example filter chains (grade.py has --list-presets; use them as starting points or mix your own):

  • warm_cinematic — retro/technical, subtle teal/orange split, desaturated. Shipped in a real launch video. Safe for talking heads.
  • neutral_punch — minimal corrective: contrast bump + gentle S-curve. No hue shifts.
  • none — straight copy. Default when the user hasn't asked.

For anything else — portraiture, nature, product, music video, documentary — invent your own chain. grade.py --filter '<raw ffmpeg>' accepts any filter string.

Hard rules: apply per-segment during extraction (not post-concat, which re-encodes twice). Never go aggressive without testing skin tones.

Subtitles (when requested)

Subtitles have three dimensions worth reasoning about: chunking (1/2/3/sentence per line), case (UPPER/Title/Natural), and placement (margin from bottom). The right combo depends on content.

Worked styles — pick, adapt, or invent:

bold-overlay — short-form tech launch, fast-paced social. 2-word chunks, UPPERCASE, break on punctuation, Helvetica 18 Bold, white-on-outline, MarginV=35. render.py ships with this as SUB_FORCE_STYLE.

FontName=Helvetica,FontSize=18,Bold=1,
PrimaryColour=&H00FFFFFF,OutlineColour=&H00000000,BackColour=&H00000000,
BorderStyle=1,Outline=2,Shadow=0,
Alignment=2,MarginV=35

natural-sentence (if you invent this mode) — narrative, documentary, education. 4–7 word chunks, sentence case, break on natural pauses, MarginV=60–80, larger font for readability, slightly wider max-width. No shipped force_style — design one if you need it.

Invent a third style if neither fits. Hard rules: subtitles LAST (Rule 1), output-timeline offsets (Rule 5).

Animations (when requested)

Animations match the content and the brand. Get the palette, font, and visual language from the conversation — never assume a default. If the user hasn't told you, propose a palette in the strategy phase and wait for confirmation before building anything.

Tool options:

Pick the engine per animation slot. Do not default to Remotion just because the animation is web-adjacent.

  • HyperFrames — Browser-native HTML/CSS/GSAP video compositions: product UI motion, website-to-video or mockup-to-video captures, kinetic typography, landing-page/storyboard promos, data-driven UI states, transparent WebM overlays, and clips that need deterministic frame capture plus HyperFrames lint/validate/render checks. Best when the animation should be authored and verified like a web composition instead of a React component tree.
  • Remotion — React/CSS compositions with component state, reusable React primitives, or an existing Remotion brand system. Best when the user specifically asks for React/Remotion or when React composition is the simpler authoring model.
  • Manim — formal diagrams, state machines, equation derivations, graph morphs. Read skills/manim-video/SKILL.md and its references for depth.
  • PIL + PNG sequence + ffmpeg — simple overlay cards: counters, typewriter text, single bar reveals, progressive draws. Fast to iterate, any aesthetic you want. The launch video used this.

For HyperFrames slots, scaffold the slot inside edit/animations/slot_<id>/ with npx --yes hyperframes init . --example blank --non-interactive --skip-skills, build the HTML composition there, run the HyperFrames checks that fit the slot (lint, validate, and a draft render when practical), then produce the final overlay video with npx --yes hyperframes render . -o render.mp4 or --format webm -o render.webm when alpha is required. Point the EDL overlay file at the actual rendered path.

For Remotion slots, keep the Remotion project isolated inside the same slot directory, scaffold with npx create-video@latest or install Remotion locally there, render the composition to render.mp4 with the project-local remotion render command, and verify duration and dimensions with ffprobe.

None is mandatory. Invent hybrids if useful (e.g., PIL background with a HyperFrames or Remotion layer on top).

Duration rules of thumb, context-dependent:

  • Sync-to-narration explanations. A viewer needs to parse the content at 1×. Rough floor 3s, typical 5–7s for simple cards, 8–14s for complex diagrams. The launch video shipped at 5–7s per simple card.
  • Beat-synced accents (music video, fast montage). 0.5–2s is fine — they're visual accents, not information. The "readable at 1×" rule becomes "recognizable at 1×", not "fully parseable."
  • Hold the final frame ≥ 1s before the cut (universal).
  • Over voiceover: total duration ≥ narration_length + 1s (universal).
  • Never parallel-reveal independent elements — the eye can't track two new things at once. One thing, pause, next thing.

Animation payoff timing (rule for sync-to-narration): get the payoff word's timestamp. Start the overlay reveal_duration seconds earlier so the landing frame coincides with the spoken payoff word. Without this sync the animation feels disconnected.

Easing (universal — never linear, it looks robotic):

def ease_out_cubic(t):    return 1 - (1 - t) ** 3
def ease_in_out_cubic(t):
    if t < 0.5: return 4 * t ** 3
    return 1 - (-2 * t + 2) ** 3 / 2

ease_out_cubic for single reveals (slow landing). ease_in_out_cubic for continuous draws.

Typing text anchor trick: center on the FULL string's width, not the partial-string width — otherwise text slides left during reveal.

Example palette (the launch video — one aesthetic among infinite):

  • Background (10, 10, 10) near-black
  • Accent #FF5A00 / (255, 90, 0) orange
  • Labels (110, 110, 110) dim gray
  • Font: Menlo Bold at /System/Library/Fonts/Menlo.ttc (index 1)
  • ≤ 2 accent colors, ~40% empty space, minimal chrome
  • Result: terminal / retro tech feel

This is one style. If the brand is warm and serif, use that. If it's colorful and playful, use that. If the user handed you a style guide, follow it. If they didn't, propose one and confirm.

Parallel sub-agent brief — each animation is one sub-agent spawned via the Agent tool. Each prompt is self-contained (sub-agents have no parent context). Include:

  1. One-sentence goal: "Build ONE animation: [spec]. Nothing else."
  2. Absolute output path (<edit>/animations/slot_<id>/render.mp4)
  3. Exact technical spec: resolution, fps, codec, pix_fmt, CRF, duration
  4. Style palette as concrete values (RGB tuples, hex, or reference to a design system)
  5. Font path with index
  6. Frame-by-frame timeline (what happens when, with easing)
  7. Anti-list ("no chrome, no extras, no titles unless specified")
  8. Code pattern reference (copy helpers inline, don't import across slots)
  9. Deliverable checklist (script, render, verify duration via ffprobe, report)
  10. "Do not ask questions. If anything is ambiguous, pick the most obvious interpretation and proceed."

One sub-agent = one file (unique filenames, parallel agents don't overwrite each other).

Output spec

Match the source unless the user asked for something specific. Common targets: 1920×1080@24 cinematic, 1920×1080@30 screen content, 1080×1920@30 vertical social, 3840×2160@24 4K cinema, 1080×1080@30 square. render.py defaults the scale to 1080p from any source; pass --filter or edit the extract command for other targets. Worth asking the user which delivery format matters.

EDL format

{
  "version": 1,
  "sources": {"C0103": "/abs/path/C0103.MP4", "C0108": "/abs/path/C0108.MP4"},
  "ranges": [
    {"source": "C0103", "start": 2.42, "end": 6.85,
     "beat": "HOOK", "quote": "...", "reason": "Cleanest delivery, stops before slip at 38.46."},
    {"source": "C0108", "start": 14.30, "end": 28.90,
     "beat": "SOLUTION", "quote": "...", "reason": "Only take without the false start."}
  ],
  "grade": "warm_cinematic",
  "overlays": [
    {"file": "edit/animations/slot_1/render.mp4", "start_in_output": 0.0, "duration": 5.0}
  ],
  "subtitles": "edit/master.srt",
  "total_duration_s": 87.4
}

grade is a preset name or raw ffmpeg filter. overlays are rendered animation clips. subtitles is optional and applied LAST.

Memory — project.md

Append one section per session at <edit>/project.md:

## Session N — YYYY-MM-DD

**Strategy:** one paragraph describing the approach
**Decisions:** take choices, cuts, grades, animations + why
**Reasoning log:** one-line rationale for non-obvious decisions
**Outstanding:** deferred items

On startup, read project.md if it exists and summarize the last session in one sentence before asking whether to continue.

Anti-patterns

Things that consistently fail regardless of style:

  • Hierarchical pre-computed codec formats with USABILITY / tone tags / shot layers. Over-engineering. Derive from the transcript at decision time.
  • Hand-tuned moment-scoring functions. The LLM picks better than any heuristic you'll write.
  • Whisper SRT / phrase-level output. Loses sub-second gap data. Always word-level verbatim.
  • Running Whisper locally on CPU. Slow and it normalizes fillers. Use hosted Scribe.
  • Burning subtitles into base before compositing overlays. Overlays hide them. (Hard Rule 1.)
  • Single-pass filtergraph when you have overlays. Double re-encodes. Use per-segment extract → concat.
  • Linear animation easing. Looks robotic. Always cubic.
  • Hard audio cuts at segment boundaries. Audible pops. (Hard Rule 3.)
  • Typing text centered on the partial string. Text slides left as it grows.
  • Sequential sub-agents for multiple animations. Always parallel.
  • Editing before confirming the strategy. Never.
  • Re-transcribing cached sources. Immutable outputs of immutable inputs.
  • Assuming what kind of video it is. Look first, ask second, edit last.

# Perfil de edição — talking head (padrão Rafael)

Perfil aprovado em **2026-08-08**, derivado do edit `Teste 01 - Sem legenda.mp4`.
**Este é o padrão para todos os próximos vídeos deste diretório.** Aplique sem
perguntar; só levante a mão quando o material violar uma das premissas listadas
em "Quando este perfil NÃO se aplica".

**O que é fixo e o que é derivado — leia antes de tudo:**

| | fixo (copie) | derivado (recalcule por vídeo) |
|---|---|---|
| **Grade** | o filtro N2 | reconfirme as medições na fonte nova (§1) |
| **Corte** | só silêncio, nunca palavra (§2) | quais silêncios, por waveform |
| **Zoom** | princípios §3.1 + punch-in §3.3 | **todos os pontos e níveis** (§3.2) |
| **Overlays** | vidro, posição, geometria, animação, ícones §4.4 | **todo o texto**, e quantos slots |

O erro clássico é copiar número em vez de método. **Se você se pegar reaproveitando
um valor de zoom ou uma frase de overlay deste edit, parou errado.**

Leia junto: `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` (video-use + HyperFrames, restrições da máquina).

**Implementação de referência: `FPC_Camisa1/v02/edit/`** — `build.py` (extração
vídeo/áudio separada, composite fosco) e `edl.json` (ranges com punch-in).
Ferramentas em `tools/`: `cuts.py` (janelas de corte seguras, condição dupla) e
`silence.py` (diagnóstico do envelope, para calibrar o limiar).

---

## 1. Color grade — NEUTRO, sem push de calor

```
colorchannelmixer=rr=0.928:gg=1.039:bb=0.942
```

Vai no campo `grade` do `edl.json`. O pipeline aplica por segmento, **antes** do
composite — os overlays nunca são graduados junto.

**O diagnóstico que gerou isso** (não repita a análise do zero, mas *reconfirme os
números* em cada fonte nova — set e câmera podem mudar):

| região | R/G | B/G | alvo |
|---|---|---|---|
| pele (bochecha) | 1,551 | **0,990** | ~1,30 / ~0,86 |
| mesa (madeira clara) | 1,056 | 0,906 | ~1,10 / ~0,85 |
| laptop (cinza) | 1,021 | 1,135 | ~1,00 / ~1,00 |

O desvio **não era calor/âmbar**. Azul alto *junto* com vermelho alto = **verde em
falta** → dominante **magenta/rosada**, que lê como "pele bronzeada". Mesa e parede
já estavam quase neutras.

**Erro a não cometer:** "esfriar" a imagem (baixar R / subir B). Isso piora a pele
(o azul já está alto) e joga a madeira do set pro verde.

**Força: 70% da neutralização total, deliberadamente.** Pele e madeira ocupam a
mesma faixa tonal, então não dá pra corrigir uma sem tocar na outra:

| | pele R/G | pele B/G | mesa R/G |
|---|---|---|---|
| sem grade | 1,603 | 0,983 | 1,056 |
| **N2 = 70% (aprovado)** | **1,433** | **0,886** | 0,950 |
| N3 = 100% | 1,256 | 0,835 | 0,888 ← set esverdeado |

A 100% a pele bate o alvo exato, mas o set inteiro lê verde. 70% é o ponto de
equilíbrio. Variantes medidas, se precisar ajustar:

- N1 (40%) — `rr=0.959:gg=1.022:bb=0.967`
- N3 (100%) — `rr=0.898:gg=1.055:bb=0.917,eq=saturation=0.93`

**Sem saturação, sem contraste, sem look.** É correção, não estilo.

### Como reconfirmar numa fonte nova
Meça R/G e B/G em três regiões (pele, um cinza neutro em cena, uma superfície de
madeira/parede) na média de ~5 frames. Se a pele vier com B/G ≳ 0,95, é a mesma
dominante magenta e este grade serve. Se vier B/G ≲ 0,80, o problema é outro —
**pare e meça de novo antes de aplicar qualquer coisa.**

  afundam abaixo do limiar.

A interseção descartou exatamente esses dois cortes. Script: `cuts.py`.

**O limiar é relativo, não absoluto.** O ganho de gravação varia entre tomadas.
Meça os percentis do envelope: fala ≈ p85, room tone ≈ p05. No v02 deu −17,7 e
−46,7 dBFS, e o limiar ficou em **−42 dBFS** (~5 dB acima do room tone). Não há
separação bimodal limpa — não espere encontrar uma.

Regras operacionais:

- **Piso duro: só corte silêncio ≥ 0,15 s.** Nada menor que isso foi cortado.
- **Nunca corte dentro de palavra falada.** Confirme pelo **waveform**, não pelo
  timestamp do ASR. No edit aprovado o corte de 42,765→43,315 cai *dentro* do span
  que o Scribe deu pra "passagens." — mas a janela mede **−56,4 dB médio / −44,7 dB
  pico** (room tone puro) e a palavra termina antes. Timestamps do Scribe esticam
  no fim de frase; **verifique o nível antes de confiar.**
- **Silêncio ≥ 0,15 s é *candidato*, não gatilho automático.** Dos ~21 silêncios
  nessa faixa, 13 foram cortados e 8 mantidos. Não existe threshold puro aqui.
- **Onde cortar:** fronteira retórica — fim de frase, antes de virar de ideia,
  antes/depois de citação. Os cortes aprovados caem em: depois de `frase:`, dentro
  e depois da citação, depois de `É curioso,`, `vê`, `pronto,`, `migratória,`,
  `sazonalidade,`, `decisões`, `que`, `errado.`, `falha,`, `emitir`.
- **Onde NÃO cortar: dentro de enumeração.** O bloco de enumeração (seg07)
  preservou 4 pausas de 0,16–0,20 s de propósito — a cadência da lista é o efeito.
  Cortar ali destrói o ritmo.
- Pausa estrutural (troca de bloco) pode levar corte maior: 0,55–0,57 s.

Faixas observadas: cortado 0,150–0,570 s · preservado 0,120–0,200 s. **As faixas se
sobrepõem — por isso a escolha é editorial, não aritmética.**

### Filler — não caçar, não cortar

**Não rode scanner de filler e não proponha corte de palavra.** Coberto pela regra
fixa acima.

Vale saber por que a regra é essa: no edit aprovado, os 4 "candidatos a filler" que
um scanner acusaria eram **todos conteúdo legítimo** — `né?"` (a piada central do
vídeo), `É` de "É curioso", `é` de "que é insuficiente", `bom` de "um bom agente".
Um corte automático de filler teria destruído a peça. Detector de filler não
entende retórica; o usuário já entendeu, antes de mandar o arquivo.

### Bordas e handles
- Entrada: ~0,05 s antes da primeira palavra.
- Saída: ~0,26 s depois da última palavra (deixa a frase respirar).
- Cada segmento leva fade de áudio de **30 ms** nas duas bordas (evita estalo no
  corte). Já está em `extract_segment_zoom`.

  **libera a faixa superior pros overlays**. Zoom e overlay dependem um do outro:

"""Pipeline de render com zoom por segmento.

Reaproveita a logica auditada do render.py do video-use (concat lossless,
PTS-shift dos overlays, legenda POR ULTIMO, loudnorm) e substitui apenas a
extracao de segmento por uma versao com punch-in.

Nao modifica nada dentro do repo video-use.

Uso:
    ~/Developer/video-use/.venv/bin/python build.py -o preview.mp4 --preview
    ~/Developer/video-use/.venv/bin/python build.py -o final.mp4
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import subprocess
import sys
from pathlib import Path

HELPERS = Path.home() / "Developer" / "video-use" / "helpers"
sys.path.insert(0, str(HELPERS))

from render import (                      # noqa: E402
    concat_segments, build_master_srt, build_final_composite,
    apply_loudnorm_two_pass, is_hdr_source, TONEMAP_CHAIN, resolve_grade_filter,
)

EDIT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent

# Fonte 2160x3840 -> saida 1080x1920. Ha 2x de folga de resolucao, entao
# qualquer zoom <= 2.0 e recorte de pixel nativo: matematicamente lossless.
SRC_W, SRC_H = 2160, 3840
OUT_W, OUT_H = 1080, 1920

# Enquadramento: o plano tem muito espaco morto acima da cabeca. Puxar o
# recorte para cima (0.28) tira mais mesa que testa E abaixa o sujeito no
# quadro, liberando a faixa superior para os overlays nos segmentos com zoom.
Y_BIAS = 0.28

# Escada de zoom, um nivel por segmento. Troca sempre NA emenda do corte.
ZOOM = [
    # Escada DERIVADA deste roteiro (perfil §3.2). Emenda "punch" nao remove
    # tempo: e so troca de escala sobre a mesma tomada continua.
    1.00,  # 00  abertura — override do perfil, sempre 1.00
    1.14,  # 01  PUNCH  "alguem decreta o fim das agencias"
    1.06,  # 02  enumeracao (carrega slot_1) — base larga, a lista precisa de ar
    1.16,  # 03  PUNCH  "e agora a bola da vez e a inteligencia artificial"
    1.04,  # 04  volta  "O curioso e"
    1.12,  # 05  PUNCH  "o mercado continua crescendo"
    1.08,  # 06  "nao porque as pessoas precisam de mais informacao"
    1.18,  # 07  PUNCH  "informacao nunca foi tao abundante"
    1.06,  # 08  volta  "conhecimento, experiencia e lastro"
    1.20,  # 09  PUNCH  "a distinguir, a decidir, a bater o martelo"
    1.14,  # 10  tese   "elimina a tarefa, mas exige julgamento" (carrega slot_2)
    1.24,  # 11  PUNCH CLIMAX  "meu amigo, minha amiga, so TBC que traz"
    1.00,  # 12  payoff — reentrada limpa depois da pausa estrutural de 0.56s
    1.12,  # 13  PUNCH  "se vender decisao, vai lucrar"
]


def _has_libass() -> bool:
    """O filtro `subtitles` so existe se o ffmpeg foi compilado com libass."""
    out = subprocess.run(["ffmpeg", "-hide_banner", "-filters"],
                         capture_output=True, text=True).stdout
    return any(line.split()[1:2] == ["subtitles"] for line in out.splitlines() if line.split())


def seg_real_duration(path: Path) -> float:
    """Duracao REAL do segmento extraido (o ffmpeg arredonda para frame inteiro)."""
    out = subprocess.run(["ffprobe", "-v", "error", "-show_entries", "format=duration",
                          "-of", "default=nw=1:nk=1", str(path)],
                         capture_output=True, text=True).stdout.strip()
    return float(out)


def src_to_real_out(t: float, ranges: list, real: list[float]) -> float:
    """Mapeia tempo da FONTE -> tempo de saida usando as duracoes MEDIDAS.

    Ancorar overlay em tempo de saida planejado acumula o arredondamento de
    frame de cada segmento (~+0.28s ao longo de 14 cortes…

    return acc


def even(n: float) -> int:
    return int(n) // 2 * 2


def zoom_chain(z: float) -> str:
    """crop centrado (com vies vertical) + scale para a saida."""
    cw, ch = even(SRC_W / z), even(SRC_H / z)
    x = even((SRC_W - cw) / 2)
    y = even((SRC_H - ch) * Y_BIAS)
    return f"crop={cw}:{ch}:{x}:{y},scale={OUT_W}:{OUT_H}"


def extract_video_segment(source, seg_start, duration, zoom, grade_filter,
                          out_path, preview=False):
    """Extrai UM range de video (sem audio) no seu nivel de zoom.

    Video-only de proposito: com punch-in, a maioria das emendas nao remove
    tempo — sao so trocas de escala. Se o audio viajasse junto com cada range,
    cada punch viraria uma emenda de audio (fade de 30ms de cada lado = entalhe
    de 60ms no meio de palavra, alem do erro de quantizacao do AAC). O audio e
    montado a parte, em `extract_audio_segment`, cortado so nos silencios reais.
    """
    out_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    vf_parts = []
    if is_hdr_source(source):
        vf_parts.append(TONEMAP_CHAIN)
    vf_parts.append(zoom_chain(zoom))
    if grade_filter:
        vf_parts.append(grade_filter)
    vf = ",".join(vf_parts)

    preset, crf = ("medium", "22") if preview else ("fast", "20")

    cmd = [
        "ffmpeg", "-y",
        "-ss", f"{seg_start:.4f}", "-i", str(source), "-t", f"{duration:.4f}",
        "-an", "-vf", vf,
        "-c:v", "libx264", "-preset", preset, "-crf", crf,
        "-pix_fmt", "yuv420p", "-r", "30",
        "-movflags", "+faststart", str(out_path),
    ]
    subprocess.run(cmd, check=True, stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL, stderr=subprocess.PIPE)


def extract_audio_segment(source, seg_start, duration, out_path):
    """Extrai UM bloco de audio, com o fade de 30ms em cada borda.

    Um bloco = varios ranges de video contiguos. Ha um bloco por trecho entre
    silencios REMOVIDOS, entao o fade so acontece onde de fato houve corte.
    """
    out_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    fade_out = max(0.0, duration - 0.03)
    af = f"afade=t=in:st=0:d=0.03,afade=t=out:st={fade_out:.3f}:d=0.03"
    cmd = [
        "ffmpeg", "-y",
        "-ss", f"{seg_start:.4f}", "-i", str(source), "-t", f"{duration:.4f}",
        "-vn", "-af", af,
        "-c:a", "pcm_s16le", "-ar", "48000", "-ac", "2", str(out_path),
    ]
    subprocess.run(cmd, check=True, stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL, stderr=subprocess.PIPE)


def concat_stream(paths, out_path, edit_dir, copy=True):
    """Concat demuxer sobre uma lista de arquivos homogeneos."""
    lst = edit_dir / f"_concat_{out_path.stem}.txt"
    lst.write_text("".join(f"file '{p.resolve()}'\n" for p in paths))
    cmd = ["ffmpeg", "-y", "-f", "concat", "-safe", "0", "-i", str(lst)]
    cmd += ["-c", "copy"] if copy else ["-c:a", "pcm_s16le"]
    cmd += [str(out_path)]
    subprocess.run(cmd, check=True, stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL, stderr=subprocess.PIPE)
    lst.unlink(missing_ok=True)


def audio_blocks(ranges, eps=1e-4):
    """Agrupa ranges contiguos. Emenda contigua = punch-in (nao corta audio)."""
    blocks = [[float(ranges[0]["start"]), float(ranges[0]["end"])]]
    for r in ranges[1:]:
        s, e = float(r["start"]), float(r["end"])
        if abs(s - blocks[-1][1]) < eps:
            blocks[-1][1] = e
        else:
            blocks.append([s, e])
    return blocks

def main() -> None:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("-o", "--output", type=Path, required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--preview", action="store_true")
    ap.add_argument("--no-loudnorm", action="store_true")
    # Sem isto os overlays de vidro ficam TRANSPARENTES em vez de foscos.
    ap.add_argument("--no-glass", action="store_true",
                    help="composite simples, sem o backdrop-blur dos overlays")
    # O ffmpeg do Homebrew (formula 'ffmpeg') e compilado SEM libass, entao o
    # filtro `subtitles` nao existe e o composite falha. Precisa de
    # `brew install ffmpeg-full` para queimar legenda.
    ap.add_argument("--no-subtitles", action="store_true")
    args = ap.parse_args()

    if not args.no_subtitles and not _has_libass():
        sys.exit("ffmpeg sem libass: filtro `subtitles` indisponivel.\n"
                 "  -> brew install ffmpeg-full   (ou rode com --no-subtitles)")

    edl = json.loads((EDIT / "edl.json").read_text())
    ranges = edl["ranges"]
    sources = edl["sources"]
    out_path = args.output.resolve()

    if len(ZOOM) != len(ranges):
        sys.exit(f"ZOOM tem {len(ZOOM)} niveis para {len(ranges)} segmentos")

    grade = resolve_grade_filter(edl.get("grade"))
    if grade == "__AUTO__":
        grade = ""

    clips_dir = EDIT / ("clips_preview" if args.preview else "clips_graded")
    clips_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    src = Path(sources[ranges[0]["source"]])

    # ---- VIDEO: um clipe por nivel de zoom -------------------------------
    seg_paths = []
    print(f"extraindo {len(ranges)} ranges de video")
    prev_end = None
    for i, r in enumerate(ranges):
        start, end = float(r["start"]), float(r["end"])
        dur = end - start
        kind = "punch " if prev_end is not None and abs(start - prev_end) < 1e-4 else "CORTE "
        out = clips_dir / f"seg_{i:02d}.mp4"
        print(f"  [{i:02d}] {kind} {start:7.3f}-{end:7.3f} ({dur:5.2f}s)  "
              f"zoom {ZOOM[i]:.2f}x   {r.get('beat','')}")
        extract_video_segment(src, start, dur, ZOOM[i], grade, out, preview=args.preview)
        seg_paths.append(out)
        prev_end = end

    vbase = EDIT / "_video_base.mp4"
    concat_stream(seg_paths, vbase, EDIT)

    # ---- AUDIO: um bloco por trecho entre silencios REMOVIDOS ------------
    blocks = audio_blocks(ranges)
    print(f"extraindo {len(blocks)} blocos de audio "
          f"({len(ranges) - len(blocks)} emendas de punch nao tocam no audio)")
    apaths = []
    for i, (s, e) in enumerate(blocks):
        print(f"  [{i:02d}] {s:7.3f}-{e:7.3f} ({e - s:5.2f}s)")
        out = clips_dir / f"aud_{i:02d}.wav"
        extract_audio_segment(src, s, e - s, out)
        apaths.append(out)
    abase = EDIT / "_audio_base.wav"
    concat_stream(apaths, abase, EDIT, copy=False)

    base = EDIT / ("base_preview.mp4" if args.preview else "base.mp4")
    subprocess.run(["ffmpeg", "-y", "-i", str(vbase), "-i", str(abase),
                    "-map", "0:v", "-map", "1:a", "-c:v", "copy",
                    "-c:a", "aac", "-b:a", "192k", "-ar", "48000",
                    "-shortest", …

saída
"""Deriva janelas de corte seguras — perfil FPC, CLAUDE.md §2.

REGRA FIXA: so silencio, nunca palavra. Aqui isso vira uma condicao DUPLA —
uma janela so e cortavel se for, ao mesmo tempo:

  (a) silencio no WAVEFORM  (envelope RMS abaixo do limiar), e
  (b) gap entre palavras no ASR.

Nenhum dos dois sozinho basta:
  - So ASR falha: no v02 o "gap" entre 'so' e 'TBC' tem pico de -0.7 dBFS.
  - So waveform falha: o detector achou 0.22s de silencio EM CIMA da palavra
    'vez' (fricativa fraca entre duas vogais curtas) e 0.24s sobre 'so'.

A intersecao mata os dois erros. Ainda encolhe GUARD em cada borda, para o corte
nunca encostar no ataque da proxima palavra.

O limiar e RELATIVO ao proprio video (o ganho de gravacao varia entre tomadas):
por padrao room-tone (p05 do envelope) + MARGIN dB.

Uso: cuts.py <video> <transcript.json> [--margin 5] [--floor 0.15] [--guard 0.03]
"""
import argparse, json, subprocess
import numpy as np
from pathlib import Path

SR = 16000
HOP = 0.020


def load_audio(src):
    p = subprocess.run(["ffmpeg", "-v", "error", "-i", src, "-map", "0:a", "-ac", "1",
                        "-ar", str(SR), "-f", "s16le", "-"], capture_output=True, check=True)
    return np.frombuffer(p.stdout, dtype=np.int16).astype(np.float32) / 32768.0


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("video"); ap.add_argument("transcript")
    ap.add_argument("--margin", type=float, default=5.0,
                    help="dB acima do room tone que ainda conta como silencio")
    ap.add_argument("--thr", type=float, default=None, help="limiar absoluto (dBFS)")
    ap.add_argument("--floor", type=float, default=0.15)
    ap.add_argument("--guard", type=float, default=0.03)
    ap.add_argument("--out", default="/tmp/_cuts.json")
    a = ap.parse_args()

    audio = load_audio(a.video)
    n = int(HOP * SR); m = len(audio) // n
    env = 20 * np.log10(np.sqrt((audio[:m * n].reshape(m, n) ** 2).mean(1) + 1e-12))

    speech, room = np.percentile(env, 85), np.percentile(env, 5)
    thr = a.thr if a.thr is not None else room + a.margin
    print(f"fala (p85) {speech:6.1f} dBFS | room tone (p05) {room:6.1f} dBFS "
          f"| limiar {thr:6.1f} dBFS")

    q = env < thr
    runs, i = [], 0
    while i < len(q):
        if q[i]:
            j = i
            while j < len(q) and q[j]:
                j += 1
            if (j - i) * HOP >= a.floor:
                runs.append((i * HOP, j * HOP))
            i = j
        else:
            i += 1

    W = [w for w in json.loads(Path(a.transcript).read_text())["words"]
         if w.get("type") == "word"]
    gaps = [(x["end"], y["start"], i) for i, (x, y) in enumerate(zip(W, W[1:]))
            if y["start"] > x["end"]]
    print(f"silencio no waveform: {len(runs)} | gaps no ASR: {len(gaps)}\n")
    print(f"{'#':>3} {'janela de corte':>17} {'dur':>6}  antes | depois")

    out = []
    for gs, ge, gi in gaps:
        best = None
        for rs, re_ in runs:
            s, e = max(gs, rs), min(ge, re_)

"""§3.2 — mede enfase (pico de audio + energia de movimento) ao longo do video.

Gera CANDIDATOS. Quem decide sao os principios do §3.1 e a semantica do
transcript: "a medicao gera candidatos, os principios decidem".

    tools/emphasis.py <fonte.mp4> <transcript.json> [--win 4]
"""
from __future__ import annotations
import argparse, json, re, subprocess, sys
from pathlib import Path
import numpy as np

SR, FPS = 16000, 30


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("src")
    ap.add_argument("transcript")
    ap.add_argument("--win", type=float, default=4.0, help="janela do resumo (s)")
    a = ap.parse_args()

    p = subprocess.run(["ffmpeg", "-v", "error", "-i", a.src, "-map", "0:a", "-ac", "1",
                        "-ar", str(SR), "-f", "s16le", "-"], capture_output=True, check=True)
    au = np.frombuffer(p.stdout, dtype=np.int16).astype(np.float32) / 32768.0

    p2 = subprocess.run(
        ["ffmpeg", "-hide_banner", "-i", a.src, "-vf",
         "scale=240:-1,tblend=all_mode=difference,signalstats,"
         "metadata=print:key=lavfi.signalstats.YAVG", "-f", "null", "-"],
        capture_output=True, text=True)
    yavg = np.array([float(m) for m in re.findall(r"YAVG=([0-9.]+)", p2.stderr)])

    W = [w for w in json.loads(Path(a.transcript).read_text())["words"]
         if w.get("type") == "word"]
    dur = len(au) / SR

    T = np.arange(0.0, dur - 0.7, 0.25)
    apk, mot = [], []
    for t in T:
        w = au[int(t * SR):int((t + 0.7) * SR)]
        apk.append(20 * np.log10(np.abs(w).max() + 1e-6) if len(w) else -90)
        k0, k1 = int(t * FPS), int((t + 0.7) * FPS)
        mot.append(yavg[k0:k1].mean() if k1 <= len(yavg) and k1 > k0 else 0.0)
    apk, mot = np.array(apk), np.array(mot)

    z = lambda x: (x - np.percentile(x, 10)) / (np.percentile(x, 95) - np.percentile(x, 10) + 1e-9)
    score = np.clip(z(apk), 0, 1.4) + np.clip(z(mot), 0, 1.4)

    print(f"duracao {dur:.2f}s · audio pico p50 {np.percentile(apk,50):.1f} "
          f"p95 {np.percentile(apk,95):.1f} dBFS · movimento p50 "
          f"{np.percentile(mot,50):.2f} p95 {np.percentile(mot,95):.2f}")
    print(f"\n{'janela':>13} {'score':>6} {'@':>7} {'aud':>6} {'mov':>5}  texto")
    for t0 in np.arange(0, dur, a.win):
        m = (T >= t0) & (T < t0 + a.win)
        if not m.any():
            continue
        i = int(np.argmax(np.where(m, score, -9)))
        txt = " ".join(w["text"] for w in W if t0 <= w["start"] < t0 + a.win)[:58]
        bar = "#" * int(score[m].max() * 10)

This confirms measurement only generates candidates; semantic judgment has to override it and drive the zoom ladder.

I'm mapping out the semantic hierarchy: the climax thesis line, the historical payoff about the 400 agencies, the provocation questions, the turning point with Galileu, and the proposal with the machines and reservations. Since the user wants at least 25% zoom on emphasis moments, I'll set those key points to 1.25× while reserving the single highest peak for the true climax, consistent with the idea that there's only one climax.

I'm now working out the zoom levels: the lone climax gets 1.32×, intermediate punches sit between 1.08-1.16×, and the base stays around 1.00-1.05×. Since 1.32× on a 1080p source means cropping and upscaling, I'm flagging that as noticeably soft but acceptable on a phone screen with lanczos scaling.

For the jump cuts, I'm balancing apparent head size across transitions — giving the climax segment a high zoom before resetting to 1.00 at the next structural pause, and compensating the D-to-E transition with a higher zoom on E since he appears smaller there, while also considering the CTA's framing.

Now I'm thinking through the overlay titles — the user wants a Liquid Glass style title in the opening seconds reflecting the video's theme, plus highlights using HyperFrames Liquid Glass. Given the video's focus on the Galileu system and "cada geração tem o seu mensageiro," I'm drafting a title card with that phrase as the headline and a subtitle tying it to what the system teaches about 2026.

I'm also mapping out the other overlay slots: one showing the old manual process stats (messengers, phone calls, half a day per sale) around 48-56s, a lower-third identity card for Maria Luiza Rodenburg and the Galileu system around 60-66s, a proposal comparison (machines/installation/training vs. monthly bookings) around 80-92s, and a payoff stat about agency growth over two years.

Now I'm weighing whether 6 overlay slots is too many given the 8GB RAM constraint — compositing six 1080×1920 yuva streams could risk SIGKILL under memory pressure, especially since the reference only used 3 slots on a similar-length source. I'm leaning toward trimming down to 5 slots to stay safe.

Merging into one ProRes 4444 stream would be too large on disk, so instead I'll keep 4-5 overlay slots and let the build script handle it, falling back to a two-pass composite if it gets SIGKILLed. I'll go with 5 slots as the plan.

Now I want to write up the strategy for confirmation, flagging key issues: the source is already native resolution so any zoom would mean upscaling, there's no subtitle burn-in since ffmpeg lacks libass (I'll generate a master.srt instead), and I need to note the restructuring approach.

Checking the skin tone ratios against the pele profile targets, R/G is already within range so there's no red excess, but B/G is running high relative to the target, suggesting green is deficient compared to blue — which per the profile's logic could indicate a magenta cast forming.

Testing the N2 grade against the profile's own B/G≳0.95 criterion confirms it technically applies, but applying its correction factors would push R/G too low, making skin look too pale and green. So I'm working out a custom correction instead — nudging R/G up slightly while pulling B/G down — but I need to be careful since the whole scene is already bathed in green LED light, so boosting green further risks amplifying that cast. I'm cross-checking against the ceiling/wall reference, which reads notably warm and yellow.

Checking the shirt as a neutral reference confirms the same pattern seen in skin: red and green are balanced, but blue runs consistently high by about 5-10%, pointing to a mild cool cast from the green LED spill affecting white balance. I'm settling on a correction that pulls blue down roughly 8% while leaving red alone and trimming green slightly to preserve luminance, then verifying the resulting ratios land close to target for both shirt and skin.

Since full neutralization risks looking artificial, I'm dialing the correction back to about 70% strength, landing on a gentle blue-pull adjustment that reads as a correction rather than a stylistic look.

I'll verify this visually during execution rather than overthinking it now. Next I need to write up the strategy for the user per Hard Rule 11, noting the total runtime estimate of about 2:45 and confirming loudnorm will handle the audio level issue before presenting the plan.

Estratégia proposta

O corte. O vídeo se auto-edita: em 2:50 você fala com o editor ("vou só ajustar aqui, Solon... pra finalizar melhor a provocação") e regrava o final duas vezes. Então:

#O quêFonteAção
1Slate + "200 passageiros, mensageiros, aliás"0–6,4sfora
2Hook → história → payoff das 400 agências6,4–112smantém
3Lição + tese "cada geração tem o seu mensageiro"113,6–145,9smantém
4Provocação v1145,9–158,2sfora (você a substituiu)
5Fala com o editor + retake falho + "vou de novo"170,8–192,3sfora
6Provocação v3 — a boa, com a síndrome de Gabriela195,4–212,2sentra no lugar da v1
7Assinatura + CTA159,5–169,8svai para o fim
8"Aí segue pro final, fechou?"214–215,2sfora

Mais 2 silêncios estruturais (0,6s e 1,5s). Resultado: 3:36 → ~2:45.

Preservo de propósito as pausas curtas da enumeração ("dois, três mensageiros, quatro telefonemas") e a pausa dramática antes de "mais de quatrocentas agências" — ali a cadência é o efeito.

O zoom. Base 1,00, escada por ênfase semântica, troca sempre na emenda. Os pontos ≥25%: o payoff das 400 agências, a tese "em 2026 o mensageiro é digital" (clímax, 1,32×), e a provocação final. Uso a escada também para disfarçar as duas emendas de remontagem — em 195s você está bem mais perto da câmera, então entro baixo ali; em 159s mais recuado, então entro alto.

Os overlays. HyperFrames, receita de vidro do perfil aprovado, banda superior, composite fosco no ffmpeg — o desfoque sai do próprio frame do vídeo, exatamente como você pediu. 5 slots:

  1. Título (~7s) — "Cada geração tem o seu mensageiro" / "O que o sistema Galileu ensina sobre 2026"
  2. O custo de uma venda (~50s) — 2–3 mensageiros · 4 telefonemas · meio dia
  3. 1992 · Maria Luiza Rodenburg (~62s) — lower third
  4. A proposta (~84s) — 3 máquinas + treinamento ⇄ 500 reservas/mês
  5. O payoff (~106s) — +400 agências em 2 anos

Legenda: gero o master.srt mas não queimo — seu ffmpeg não tem libass e você prefere legendar por conta.

Dois pontos que preciso sinalizar antes de executar:

1. Não há folga de resolução para o zoom. A fonte é 1080×1920 nativo (não é 4K). No perfil do HD o zoom é lossless porque a fonte tem 2× de folga — aqui não tem. Um zoom de 1,25× recorta 864×1536 e reamplia para 1080×1920; o clímax em 1,32× recorta 818×1454. Vai haver perda real de nitidez nesses trechos. Uso lanczos para minimizar, e em tela de celular passa bem, mas não é o corte de pixel nativo do perfil.

2. O áudio já veio limitado — pico grudado em −0,0 dBFS no vídeo inteiro. Não dá para usar pico como sinal de ênfase (usei RMS), e a normalização final para −14 LUFS não vai recuperar dinâmica que já foi perdida na gravação.

{
  "command": "~/Developer/video-use/.venv/bin/python - <<'PY'\nimport subprocess, numpy as np\nW,H=1080,1920\n# amostra 1 frame a cada 4s em fps baixo, so a faixa central de colunas\np=subprocess.run([\"ffmpeg\",\"-v\",\"error\",\"-i\",\"video1.mp4\",\"-vf\",\n                  \"fps=1/4,crop=460:900:310:120,scale=115:225\",\n                  \"-f\",\"rawvideo\",\"-pix_fmt\",\"gray\",\"-\"],capture_output=True,check=True)\na=np.frombuffer(p.stdout,dtype=np.uint8)\nn=a.size//(115*225); a=a[:n*115*225].reshape(n,225,115).astype(np.float32)\nprint(f\"{n} frames amostrados\")\ntops=[]\nfor k in range(n):\n    f=a[k]\n    thr=np.percentile(f,18)+8          # cabelo = escuro\n    dark=f<thr\n    # primeira linha com >=35% de colunas escuras em 3 linhas seguidas\n    frac=dark.mean(1)\n    hit=None\n    for y in range(len(frac)-3):\n        if frac[y]>0.35 and frac[y+1]>0.35 and frac[y+2]>0.35:\n            hit=y; break\n    if hit is not None:\n        tops.append(120+hit*(900/225))   # volta pra coords da fonte\nt=np.array(tops)\nprint(f\"topo da cabeca (fonte, y px): min {t.min():.0f}  p05 {np.percentile(t,5):.0f}  \"\n      f\"mediana {np.median(t):.0f}  p95 {np.percentile(t,95):.0f}  max {t.max():.0f}\")\nprint(f\"\\n{'zoom':>5} {'crop_y':>7} {'topo na saida':>14}  banda segura ate\")\nfor z in (1.00,1.04,1.06,1.08,1.10,1.12,1.14,1.18,1.20,1.25,1.32):\n    ch=int(H/z)//2*2; y0=int((H-ch)*0.28)//2*2\n    top=(t.min()-y0)*z\n    print(f\"{z:5.2f} {y0:7d} {top:14.0f}  {max(0,top-24):.0f} px\")\nPY",

raciocínio interno

I've got concrete numbers now: the head top reaches y=312 in the source, and at zoom 1.25 the output head top sits at 258px, so a crop band from 60 to 432 clearly overlaps it. The current Y_BIAS of 0.28 doesn't leave much headroom since the subject is already positioned high in the frame, so I'm weighing whether increasing Y_BIAS to shift the crop start higher would push the subject down enough to fix this.

Working through the math, a Y_BIAS near zero is too aggressive since it crops everything off the bottom. Instead I try shrinking the band itself — top at 60px with 170px height, bottom at 230px — which gives 28px clearance at z=1.25 but only 10px at z=1.32. Since the max zoom with an overlay is actually 1.25 (slot_5, as 1.32 belongs to the climax with no overlay), this band configuration should work.

But a 170px band feels short for a title card with a subtitle, so I'm considering varying the band per slot based on each one's specific zoom level: slot_1 at z=1.00 allows a card bottom up to 288px, slot_2 at z=1.10 up to 266px, slot_3 at z=1.12 up to 263px, slot_4 at z=1.06 up to 275px, and slot_5 at z=1.25 up to 234px — all fairly tight constraints clustering around similar bounds.

Rather than fight these tight margins, I'm trying a modest Y_BIAS reduction to 0.12 to gain more room, recalculating the top-out positions for each zoom level to see how much extra band space this frees up.

At z=1.00 the top stays consistent too, confirming Y_BIAS=0.12 with a band starting at 60px and 230px tall works across zoom levels. That's tight for a title card with title and subtitle, but checking the math it should fit — though I'm leaning toward widening the band slightly, maybe starting at 44px for 246px of height, to give more breathing room. Now I want to render test frames to confirm this visually.

raciocínio interno

The video ends up 421ms longer than the audio, which matches the expected frame-rounding accumulation across 26 segments at 30fps (each rounding up adds ~16ms). Since the mux uses -shortest, the final output gets trimmed to the audio's 164.61s length even though the video stream itself ran to 165.03s.

My guard is comparing the wrong values though — it checks the video stream duration against total_real and flags an error at the 0.5s threshold, but the reference implementation had the same drift pattern (just smaller, ~0.28s) and passed fine. The real concern is whether this rounding causes actual lip-sync drift: since each segment's video is independently rounded up relative to its audio, the video content could progressively lag behind where the audio expects it to be by the end of the clip.

I'm weighing fixes — one option is extracting video segments using exact frame counts computed from duration times fps, so rounding errors tend to cancel out across segments rather than accumulate in one direction.

Quantizing each segment independently causes drift to accumulate across a block—up to 200ms of source skipped relative to audio over many segments. A better fix is to snap the range boundaries themselves to the frame grid in source time, so segments stay aligned without compounding error.

To be extra safe against ffmpeg's rounding, I should also pass -frames:v explicitly rather than relying on -t alone. I'll quantize the ranges to the 1/30 grid right in build.py when loading the EDL, snapping both start and end of each range so contiguous segments stay contiguous, then use -frames:v during extraction for exact frame counts.

Once that drift is fixed, the guard that flagged the mismatch should stop firing since video and audio lengths will align, and the -shortest truncation issue goes away too. I also want to reconsider the output frame rate: the source is 59.94fps (60000/1001), and using a flat 30fps output creates uneven 2:1 timing that can cause judder, so 29.97fps (30000/1001) would be the cleaner match.

Deciding this is worth doing properly since the drift fix needs a quantization grid anyway, so I'll switch the output to true 29.97fps and patch build.py to quantize range boundaries to frame duration, update the frame count flag, and set the color source rate to match.

Claude

Duas coisas para corrigir antes de seguir — e a segunda é séria:

Drift de sincronia. video 165.033s · audio 164.613s (delta +421 ms). Cada um dos 26 segmentos de vídeo arredonda para frame inteiro para cima, e o erro acumula: no fim do vídeo o lábio estaria ~0,42s à frente do áudio. O -shortest mascarou isso truncando a saída, e a guarda do pipeline pegou. Vou quantizar as fronteiras na grade de frame, para vídeo e áudio baterem exatamente.

Aproveito e corrijo a taxa: a fonte é 59,94fps, então saída em 29,97 é decimação limpa 2:1 (30,0 exato geraria um frame irregular a cada ~33s).

Both silence cuts look clean with consistent framing. Now I need to render the 5 HyperFrames slots to ProRes 4444 MOV with alpha, one at a time given the 8GB RAM limit, starting with slot_1 and verifying the pix_fmt comes out as yuva as required by the profile.

There's also a minor fps mismatch to consider: the composition runs at 30fps while the base video is 29.97fps, which could cause a tiny drift when overlaying, but since the overlay uses tpad with shortest=1, the ~0.1% difference over 10 seconds should be negligible and invisible in practice.

To be safe though, I'll force the overlay stream to match the base frame rate by adding an fps conversion right after the format step in the filter chain, guaranteeing alignment rather than relying on framesync to smooth it out. I'll patch build.py to add this fps conversion on the overlay inputs before rendering the slots.

Claude

The frosted glass effect looks great — the track-lighting rail stays sharp outside the panel but blurs and dissolves inside it, picking up the green and cream tones behind it, with rounded corners, a thin luminous border, and crisp legible text. This matches exactly what was requested: using the video itself as the blur background for the overlay. Now I'm moving on to run the full composite.

Since the preview already validated the approach, I'll skip straight to the final render at crf17 with the slow preset rather than previewing again — though that means a longer processing time, maybe 10-20 minutes for extraction plus compositing across 26 segments on limited memory. I'll kick this off in the background, disabling subtitles since libass isn't available, and keeping loudnorm enabled.

I'm weighing whether to batch all 5 segments in one pass versus splitting into multiple passes — doing it in two passes means two re-encodes and some modest quality loss, but one pass risks running out of memory and getting killed mid-render. I'll try the larger batch first and fall back to splitting if it fails.

Actually, a cleaner approach: separate the extraction step from the compositing step entirely. First I'll run the build script with overlays, subtitles, and loudnorm disabled to produce a clean base video file. Then I'll run the compositing as a separate step from that base file, adding all the overlays and loudnorm afterward — this way if the composite step fails, I don't have to redo the expensive extraction. Starting step one now in the background.

raciocínio interno

While the slow preset encode runs in the background, I'll write the project.md memory file and think through the final composite approach. I'm weighing whether to do the five overlays in one pass versus two passes—since two passes means re-encoding the whole video twice and losing quality, I'm looking for a way to keep it to a single pass while managing memory, since the overlays don't overlap in time.

Reconsidering the memory worry: ffmpeg streams frames rather than holding everything at once, so even with five overlays and a blend chain, buffering should stay in the hundreds of MB range, well within an 8GB machine. The earlier crash was likely caused by mismatched tpad durations causing framesync to hold excess frames, not a fundamental memory limit—so with correctly balanced tpad streams, a single five-overlay pass should work, and I'll fall back to three overlays if it fails.

raciocínio interno

Found the bug: entry 5 is missing the word "Em" from "Em algumas grandes agências." This happens because my segment-building logic filters words by strict start/end boundaries, and due to floating-point quantization, the word "Em" at the exact punch boundary (15.34) falls just outside the rounded boundary (15.348), so it gets excluded from the segment.

This is a real bug — words straddling punch boundaries get silently dropped from the SRT. I need to fix the segment assignment to use each word's midpoint falling within a range, rather than requiring full containment, so every word gets assigned to exactly one segment and none are lost. I'll also clean up the convoluted entries-accumulation logic while I'm at it.

Claude

Bug na legenda: a entrada 5 saiu "algumas grandes agências" — falta o "Em". A palavra cai exatamente na fronteira de um punch (15,34s) e meu filtro exigia a palavra inteira dentro do segmento, então ela sumiu nas duas bordas. Vou trocar por atribuição pelo ponto médio.

183	def build_srt(edl, ranges, real, out_path):
184	    """SRT no timeline de SAIDA, com as duracoes reais medidas.
185	
186	    Nao e queimado (o ffmpeg desta maquina nao tem libass) — e para o usuario
187	    importar no editor dele, entao quebra em frase legivel, nao em 2 palavras.
188	    """
189	    tr = json.loads((EDIT / "transcripts" / "video1.json").read_text())
190	    words = [w for w in tr["words"] if w.get("type") == "word"]
191	
192	    def fmt(t):
193	        h, rem = divmod(max(0.0, t), 3600)
194	        m, s = divmod(rem, 60)
195	        return f"{int(h):02d}:{int(m):02d}:{s:06.3f}".replace(".", ",")
196	
197	    entries, idx = [], 0
198	    for r, d in zip(ranges, real):
199	        a, b = float(r["start"]), float(r["end"])
200	        off = src_to_real_out(a, ranges, real)
201	        scale = d / (b - a)
202	        seg = [w for w in words if w["start"] >= a and w["end"] <= b]
203	        chunk = []
204	        for w in seg:
205	            chunk.append(w)
206	            txt = (w.get("text") or "").strip()
207	            if len(chunk) >= 6 or (txt and txt[-1] in ".,!?:;"):
208	                entries.append(chunk); chunk = []
209	        if chunk:
210	            entries.append(chunk)
211	        for c in entries[idx:]:
212	            c.append({"_off": off, "_a": a, "_scale": scale})
213	        idx = len(entries)
214	
215	    lines = []
216	    for i, c in enumerate(entries, 1):
217	        meta = c[-1]; ws = c[:-1]
218	        if not ws:
219	            continue
220	        st = meta["_off"] + (ws[0]["start"] - meta["_a"]) * meta["_scale"]
221	        en = meta["_off"] + (ws[-1]["end"] - meta["_a"]) * meta["_scale"]
222	        text = " ".join((w.get("text") or "").strip() for w in ws)
223	        lines.append(f"{i}\n{fmt(st)} --> {fmt(en)}\n{text}\n")
224	    out_path.write_text("\n".join(lines), encoding="utf-8")
225	    print(f"  master.srt: {len(lines)} legendas")
226

raciocínio interno

I'm noticing a timing discrepancy: the SRT's last entry ends at 2:22.095, but the video runs to about 2:44.6, and the final line "próximo" should land near 2:44.5 based on its source timestamp. That's a gap of over 20 seconds I need to investigate.

Tracing through the source-to-output time mapping function, I find the bug: it walks ranges assuming source times increase monotonically, but since blocks got reordered (ASSINATURA now comes before PROVOCAÇÃO in source order despite appearing earlier in output), the function hits a later range whose start time exceeds the target time and returns early, truncating the accumulated offset. This explains the missing ~22 seconds. I should check whether my overlay anchors are affected too, though they're all positioned in the earlier blocks so may have escaped the bug.

Checking the PROVOCAÇÃO block further confirms it's actually correct since its boundary aligns exactly with range 21's end. So the bug is isolated to the ASSINATURA block (ranges 24 and 25), which need their offset calculation fixed.

I'm rewriting the src_to_real_out function to scan through all ranges and find the one whose source interval actually contains the target time, rather than relying on index assumptions — this way it works correctly regardless of which block is being queried.

This is strictly better than the old version, which only handled early-return for t falling in a removed gap — now such cases fall through and return the total, which is fine since anchors always land inside kept ranges. I should fix the not-found case to return the total as a safe fallback, and make sure the fix propagates properly since composite.py imports from build.

Claude

Bug maior encontrado: a legenda termina em 2:22 num vídeo de 2:44,6. A causa é src_to_real_out — ela assume que os ranges sobem monotonicamente no tempo da fonte e retorna cedo quando t < a. Com a remontagem, a assinatura (fonte 159s) vem depois da provocação (fonte 195s), então a função abortava ali e devolvia o offset errado.

Os overlays escaparam por acaso (todas as âncoras estão antes da remontagem), mas a função está errada. Vou corrigir a busca.

O arquivo final

[oculto]/Desktop/aula-edit/edit/final.mp4

1080×1920 · 29,97fps · 2:44,6 (de 3:36) · 178 MB · áudio −14 LUFS / −1,1 dBTP.

O master.srt está ao lado, no timeline já cortado, em quebra de frase legível para você importar. Ele foi entregue aqui no chat; o vídeo tem 178 MB e passa do limite de upload, então só aparece no app desktop desta máquina — abra pelo caminho acima.

O que foi feito

O corte. Fora: slate, o "200 passageiros, mensageiros, aliás", a provocação v1, a conversa com o Solon, o retake falho, o "vou de novo" e o "aí segue pro final". A provocação v3 (com a síndrome de Gabriela) entrou no lugar da v1 e a assinatura foi para o fim. Preservei de propósito a cadência da enumeração e a pausa antes de "mais de quatrocentas agências" — ali a pausa é o efeito.

O zoom. 26 segmentos, clímax único em 1,32× em "em 2026, o mensageiro é digital", e 1,25× no payoff das 400 agências e na síndrome de Gabriela. A escada também disfarça as duas emendas da remontagem: em 195s você está bem mais perto da câmera, então entrei baixo ali; em 159s mais recuado, então entrei alto.

Os overlays. Cinco painéis de vidro, com o desfoque saindo do próprio frame — dá para ver o trilho de luz nítido fora do painel e dissolvido dentro dele.

Quatro coisas que você precisa saber

Seu áudio está gravando quente demais. Chegou aqui a −9,08 LUFS com true peak +1,34 dBTP — ou seja, já clipando na fonte. A normalização corrigiu o nível, mas o que foi perdido na gravação não volta. Vale baixar o ganho no próximo.

O zoom custou nitidez, como avisei. A fonte é 1080×1920 nativo, sem folga. Inspecionei o clímax: os fios de cabelo continuam resolvidos e em celular passa bem, mas não é o corte de pixel nativo dos vídeos 4K.

Achei dois bugs no build.py de referência do HD e corrigi aqui — os dois estão latentes lá:

  • O arredondamento de frame acumulava +421 ms de dessincronia labial ao longo dos 26 segmentos (lá dá +280 ms, logo abaixo da guarda). Corrigido para +3 ms.
  • src_to_real_out assume que os cortes sobem em ordem na fonte. Como remontei o final, ela abortava no bloco errado e a legenda terminava em 2:22 num vídeo de 2:44.

O perfil aprovado do HD não serviu direto nesta fonte. Recalibrei três coisas com medição: o Y_BIAS (0,12 em vez de 0,28 — com 0,28 a banda invadia seu cabelo a partir de 1,25×), a geometria da banda, e o grade. O grade N2 de lá teria lavado sua pele: aqui o vermelho já está certo e o desvio é azul alto do spill do LED verde. Tudo registrado em edit/project.md.

Uma nota de escopo: autorizei cortar palavra porque você pediu explicitamente neste vídeo. Pelo perfil, essa autorização não vale para o próximo — me peça de novo quando for o caso.

Pergunta 3

No vídeo que você acabou de editar, faça mais dois ajustes e gere de novo:

1) Acelere a velocidade para 1.1x (10% mais rápido), mantendo o áudio no tom natural, sem ficar "esganiçado".

2) Corrija a cor deixando os pretos mais profundos: baixe o ponto preto para pretos mais pretos, com um leve crush nas sombras, sem escurecer os tons médios nem estourar o contraste.

Me diga onde ficou o novo arquivo final.

raciocínio interno

I need to handle two changes: speeding up to 1.1x while keeping natural pitch using setpts and atempo filters, and correcting the black point to deepen shadows slightly without affecting midtones or contrast. I'm weighing whether to apply the speed change to the final composited video, since the overlays are synced to speech and would scale proportionally anyway.

But applying the grade post-composite would also grade the overlays, which the pipeline explicitly forbids — overlays must stay ungraded and are only composited after per-segment grading. So I need to apply the grade change per-segment during extraction where it already lives, and figure out where to apply speed so overlay sync isn't broken — either by re-rendering overlay timing, speeding up both base and overlays before compositing with shifted anchors, or applying speed at the very end on the composite while accepting the grading tradeoff.

I'm leaning toward re-extracting all 26 segments with the updated grade, then speeding the base after concat but before composite, scaling overlay anchor timestamps by the same factor, and re-encoding the overlay ProRes 4444 movs with adjusted timestamps to keep them in sync without quality loss.

Actually, the cleaner path is to modify the HyperFrames compositions themselves—dividing cue times and durations by 1.1 and re-rendering natively—since the overlays are anchored to spoken words and need to stay synced at the faster pace, and this avoids any frame duplication or resampling artifacts from setpts tricks.

My plan: update the grade in edl.json, introduce a speed constant of 1.1 applied during extraction (adjusting video timing, audio tempo with pitch preservation, and recalculated frame counts), re-extract all segments since the grade changed anyway, re-render the five HyperFrames slots with cues divided by 1.1, then composite everything using anchors mapped through the sped-up timeline.

Actually, applying the speed change per-segment complicates quantization since the output duration needs to align to the frame grid. A cleaner approach: keep extraction at natural speed as it currently works, concatenate into the base file, then apply the speed change once to the entire base using a single ffmpeg pass adjusting both video timing and audio tempo together.

But that adds an extra encoding generation to the pipeline. Instead, I realize I can fold the speed adjustment directly into the final composite pass itself, applying the timing shift to the base video input and tempo adjustment to its audio there, avoiding the need for a separate speed-change step since the audio gets re-encoded during loudnorm anyway.

I need to rescale everything downstream to the faster timeline — the total duration, the background color source duration, the tpad tail calculations, and the overlay anchor positions all need to be divided by the speed factor. I'm weighing whether to re-render the overlay clips at the new speed or just apply a setpts adjustment directly within the existing filter chain alongside the fps conversion already happening there.

Using setpts is simpler since it avoids re-rendering anything — the fps filter afterward will resample to 29.97 and just drop about 10% of frames, which is imperceptible for smooth animation. Re-rendering natively would be more precise but requires editing cue values across five HTML files, which is riskier, so I'll go with the setpts approach as the safer, simpler equivalent of a speed change.

Now I'm thinking through the color grade — specifically how to crush the black point without disturbing midtones, considering options like a curves adjustment, a luma LUT, or colorlevels with adjusted minimum input values.

This curve design keeps midtones and highlights untouched while deepening blacks and adding a slight shadow crush — exactly the "darker blacks, no midtone darkening" effect needed. I should first check the actual black level in the footage before locking in the 0.06 point, since the real darkest values might sit higher or lower than assumed.

I'll measure the luma histogram on base.mp4 rather than final.mp4, since that has the grade applied without overlays skewing the numbers. Once I confirm the shadow distribution, I can finalize the curve, test it on sample frames, update the EDL grade, and re-extract all 26 segments before moving on to adding speed to the composite.

def extract_audio_segment(source, seg_start, duration, out_path):
    """Um bloco de audio com fade de 30ms em cada borda (Regra Dura nº 3)."""
    out_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    out_dur = duration / SPEED
    fade_out = max(0.0, out_dur - 0.03)
    # atempo PRIMEIRO: os fades tem de cair no tempo de saida, senao o de saida
    # entra 10% cedo demais e corta a ultima silaba do bloco.
    af = ""
    if SPEED != 1.0:
        af = f"atempo={SPEED},"
    af += f"afade=t=in:st=0:d=0.03,afade=t=out:st={fade_out:.3f}:d=0.03"
    cmd = [
        "ffmpeg", "-y",
        "-ss", f"{seg_start:.6f}", "-i", str(source), "-t", f"{duration:.6f}",
        "-vn", "-af", af,
        "-c:a", "pcm_s16le", "-ar", "48000", "-ac", "2", str(out_path),
    ]
    subprocess.run(cmd, check=True, stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL, stderr=subprocess.PIPE)


def concat_stream(paths, out_path, edit_dir, copy=True):

So each block's output duration should actually be duration/1.1, meaning the total should sum to about 149.48s rather than the 164.431s that was reported, which doesn't match.

I'm now suspecting the concat step or a stale build might be the culprit, so I want to directly measure the actual wav file durations in clips_graded to verify what's really happening rather than reasoning about it abstractly.

Achei o bug, e é sério. No comando de áudio o -t vem depois do -i, então é opção de saída: o ffmpeg limitava a saída a duration segundos, mas não limitava a entrada — com atempo ele puxava duration × 1,1 de fonte para preencher. Pergunta 4

raciocínio interno

I'm working through the S-curve adjustment — deepening the blacks while also rolling off the highlights so they're less blown out, which means pulling the top of the curve down rather than the typical contrast-boosting lift.

Looking at the highlight distribution, p90 sits at 164, p99 at 178, but p99.9 jumps to 249 and max hits 254 — so only a tiny fraction (the light fixtures and specular hits) is actually clipping, while the bulk of highlights are well below that.

I suspect the real culprit is the green channel railing on the LED wall rather than overall luminance blowing out, so I want to check per-channel histograms to confirm. Separately, I need to figure out the overlay height reduction — 12% likely refers to 12% of frame height, which would shift the band top from 44 to roughly 274.

Checking head clearance, moving the band to 274 with ~200px tall cards means the band bottom lands around 520, well past the measured head top near 317px — so the cards will cover part of his hair. That's consistent with the profile's note that this is an intentional directorial choice, not a bug to fix by pushing the band back up.

The hard guard is the subtitle zone at y=1700, and with content only reaching ~500, there's plenty of margin there. Bigger fonts will push things down further though, so I should compute that and flag it, but overall the plan is clear: apply the 12% shift as requested and just note the head-covering consequence since the user already accepted this tradeoff before.

Now for the font increase, I'll bump all overlays except the title by 20-30%.

Then comes the pivotal moment — "Aí chegou um sistema chamado Galileu" — which is the turning point of the story, followed by the explanation of how the old process worked: client calls the agency, agent calls the airline, waits for confirmation, sends a messenger for the physical ticket. I'm resolving this by noting the punch-during-hold rule is the more recently approved guidance, so it should take precedence.

I'm considering locking the zoom across the whole overlay span instead, but that would mean 22.5 seconds at a single zoom level, which defeats the purpose of punches in the first place. Actual hard clipping is minimal, only 0.063% of the frame has a railed channel, mostly red. The truly bright zone above luma 200 is just 0.346%, while p90 and p99 luma sit around 164-179, showing the normal bright areas like ceiling and shirt stay well below full clipping.

What reads as "estourado" is really the lamp and ceiling hotspots — that small blob above 200 plus the flat bright ceiling area lacking detail. I want a shoulder curve that compresses roughly 0.70-1.0 to tame those blobs without greying the whole image, since the user wants recovery of blown highlights, not desaturation. I'm designing an S-curve with a deeper toe to crush shadows slightly, near-identity through midtones to preserve contrast around p50, and a gentle shoulder starting around 0.65 that compresses the top so 1.0 maps to about 0.90 — this barely touches the ceiling tones while meaningfully pulling down the blown highlight blobs.

Since the user now wants a proper S-curve but previously asked not to darken midtones, I'm keeping p50 anchored while adding a slight lift in the upper-mids for contrast, then easing into the shoulder compression toward a recessed white point around 0.92.

Checking the shape: pulling pure white down to roughly 234/255 tames specular highlights in a film-like way without affecting the ceiling tones, which sit near identity in the mid-upper range. I'm verifying the spline stays monotonic across all the anchor points before finalizing.

I'll keep the color channel mixer for the blue pull, applying it before the curves, then run a test to measure the actual percentile shifts and check a few frames to confirm the graded look holds up.

Então o ombro precisa comprimir de ~0,72 para cima, sem tocar no teto. Vou desenhar e medir.
